To exercise : sign in as admin → `/admin/webhooks` → pick scope → "New endpoint" → fill URL + subscribe to e.g. `rbac.role-assigned` → Create → copy the returned secret → wire it through your secret resolver per [docs/technical-documentation/webhook-secret-resolver.md](monark/app/docs/technical-documentation/webhook-secret-resolver.md) → trigger a matching event from another part of the admin UI → watch the delivery land in `/admin/webhooks/<id>/deliveries`.

- 2026-05-07: Module-namespace foundation + `@monark/webhooks` core module. Five-phase rollout that opens core to extension by non-core modules without modifying core code, plus a generic webhook delivery system over the same event bus.
  - **Phase 1 — Module-namespace foundation.** Feature flags + RBAC permissions + notification kinds were each closed registries (`const FLAGS`, `const PERMISSIONS`, `const NOTIFICATION_KINDS`) where adding an entry meant editing a core file. Now they're runtime registries any module registers into at api boot.
    - Migration [20260507100000_add_module_to_flags_and_permissions](monark/app/packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260507100000_add_module_to_flags_and_permissions/migration.sql) adds a `module` column to `FeatureFlag`, `FeatureFlagOverride`, and `RolePermission`. Primary key on `FeatureFlag` becomes `(module, key)` ; uniqueness on `RolePermission` becomes `(roleId, module, permission)`. Backfill : `auth.*` flags get `module='auth'`, `tenancy.*` get `module='tenancy'` ; permission slugs lose their colon prefix (`org:update-settings` → `update-settings`), with the module derived from the prefix mapping (`org:` → `organizations` for the orgs-owned set + `rbac` for the rbac-owned set, etc.).
    - [packages/feature-flags/src/contracts/flags.ts](monark/app/packages/feature-flags/src/contracts/flags.ts) replaces the static `FLAGS` const with `registerFlags(module, flags)` + `isKnownFlag(dotted)` + `parseFlagKey(dotted)` + `listFlagDescriptors()`. Call sites still use the dotted form `"<module>.<key>"` ergonomically ; internal resolution parses to the (module, key) pair.
    - [packages/rbac/src/contracts/permissions.ts](monark/app/packages/rbac/src/contracts/permissions.ts) does the same for permissions ; `validatePermissionList` now returns parsed `{ module, key }[]` pairs, `hasPermission` / `requirePermission` accept the dotted form. Categories become loose strings so an extended module can introduce its own grouping.
    - Each owning module ships a `register<Module>FeatureFlags()` / `register<Module>Permissions()` helper. [services/api/src/server.ts](monark/app/services/api/src/server.ts) calls them in deterministic order at boot, before `syncFlagsToDatabase()`.
  - **Phase 2 — Notification kinds runtime registry.** [packages/notifications/src/contracts/registry.ts](monark/app/packages/notifications/src/contracts/registry.ts) drops the static `NOTIFICATION_KINDS` const + `TEMPLATES` map for `registerNotificationKind(kind, def, messages)`. The `NotificationDataRegistry` interface stays typed via TypeScript declaration merging so per-kind payloads remain checked at call sites. `registerCoreNotificationKinds()` registers the existing eight kinds (auth._ + account._) at api boot ; extended modules register their own with the same API.
  - **Phase 3 — User / Organization metadata sidecar.** Migration [20260507110000_add_user_org_metadata_sidecar](monark/app/packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260507110000_add_user_org_metadata_sidecar/migration.sql) adds `UserMetadata` + `OrganizationMetadata` tables keyed on `(parent_id, module, key)` with a `Json` value. Generic CRUD lives under `users.metadata.{list,get,set,delete}` and `organizations.metadata.{list,get,set,delete}` tRPC procedures, gated by `users.read-metadata-for-module-<x>` / `users.write-metadata-for-module-<x>` (and the orgs equivalent) permissions that an extended module registers itself. Lets a future `@monark/posts` attach per-user preferences without touching the core schema.
  - **Phase 4 — `@monark/webhooks` core module.** Operator-registered HTTP endpoints that receive a fan-out of every domain event the platform emits. Schema in migration [20260507120000_add_webhooks](monark/app/packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260507120000_add_webhooks/migration.sql) : `WebhookEndpoint` (URL + secretHash + status + per-org or platform scope), `WebhookSubscription` (event-type filter, exact or prefix), `WebhookDelivery` (the outbox row, idempotency-keyed), `WebhookDeliveryAttempt` (per-attempt audit). The wildcard subscriber listens on the new bus `*` channel ([packages/common/src/events.ts](monark/app/packages/common/src/events.ts)) so any module's events route through webhooks the moment they're in the manifest. The worker signs the body with HMAC-SHA-256 (`Webhook-Signature: v1=<hex>`), POSTs with a 10s timeout, retries with exponential backoff (5 attempts, 30s base, 6h cap), and auto-disables endpoints after 5 consecutive failures. `webhooksRouter` exposes endpoint CRUD + secret rotation + delivery inspection + manual retry under `webhooks.*` ; `/cron/sweep-webhook-deliveries` is the external fallback for the in-process worker. The admin UI on top of this surface is a deferred follow-up — tracked in [packages/webhooks/README.md](monark/app/packages/webhooks/README.md#deferred-follow-ups).
  - **Phase 5 — Documentation.** New [docs/technical-documentation/extensibility-contract.md](monark/app/docs/technical-documentation/extensibility-contract.md) codifies what core guarantees to extended modules and what extended modules must never touch. Per-module READMEs updated to reflect the runtime registries. Manifest gains `@monark/webhooks` at tier `core`.
  - **Workspace effect.** Three new migrations, one new core package (`@monark/webhooks`), one new schema column on three existing tables, one new wildcard channel on the event bus, six new permission slugs registered at boot. Migration is non-destructive — every existing row survives, FK and unique constraints widen rather than tighten. The `services/api` boot sequence gains five `register*` calls + the webhook worker + the cron sweep endpoint.
  - **Operator handoff** : `pnpm install`. `pnpm --filter @monark/db db:migrate:dev` to apply the three migrations in order. `pnpm gen` to regenerate `events.generated.ts` (already updated for `@monark/webhooks`) and the app router composition. `pnpm --filter api dev` and check the boot log for the new `webhook delivery worker started` line. The webhook secret resolver MUST be set via `setWebhookSecretResolver()` before the worker can sign requests — see [docs/technical-documentation/webhook-secret-resolver.md](docs/technical-documentation/webhook-secret-resolver.md) for three concrete backing-store recipes (env-var JSON, per-endpoint env vars, AWS Secrets Manager / Vault) ; until one is wired, every delivery records a "no plaintext secret available" error and the endpoint auto-disables after 5 consecutive failures.
  - **Codegen tools tolerate config-only modules.** `tools/gen-events.ts` and `tools/gen-routers.ts` previously aborted when a manifest module lacked `contracts/events.ts` or `server/index.ts` ; they now skip those modules silently. Lets `@monark/branding` (constants-only) live in the manifest for the tier check without forcing it to ship empty event / router stubs.

- 2026-05-05: Test plan iteration 7. Closes the package-integration gap by landing the auth + notifications + bootstrap suites against the testcontainer Postgres, plus the two remaining admin e2e specs and a small server-side helper extraction for testability.
  - **`@monark/auth` integration suite — three specs.** Same pattern as the rbac / users / organizations suites : workspace dep on `@monark/test-utils`, dedicated [vitest.integration.config.ts](monark/app/packages/auth/vitest.integration.config.ts), opt-in `pnpm --filter @monark/auth test:integration` script.
    - [packages/auth/tests/integration/trusted-devices.test.ts](monark/app/packages/auth/tests/integration/trusted-devices.test.ts) — 23 cases. Stubs the supabase admin client at module-import boundary so `revokeTrustedDevice` doesn't try to talk to Supabase. First-time `recognizeOrRegister` (cookie hash, IP, country, label parsing, DeviceSession creation when supabaseSessionId provided + skipped when null). Cookie recognition (re-uses row, refreshes country only on fresh signal, fresh row when cookie matches a different user, fresh row when previous was revoked, idempotent DeviceSession upsert). `listTrustedDevices` (lastSeenAt-DESC ordering, scoped to userId, excludes revoked). `findCurrentDeviceId` (match / null cookie / revoked-row null). `revokeTrustedDevice` (clears DeviceSession rows, no-op on wrong-user / already-revoked, idempotent revokedAt stamp). `revokeAllTrustedDevices` (returns count, scoped to user, returns 0 on empty).
    - [packages/auth/tests/integration/totp.test.ts](monark/app/packages/auth/tests/integration/totp.test.ts) — 28 cases. Generates a per-spec `TOTP_ENCRYPTION_KEY` so the AES-256-GCM round-trip works. Stubs `@monark/feature-flags/server` so `requiresTotpChallenge` doesn't depend on a flag-eval service. `getTotpStatus` (not enrolled / mid-enrollment / activated with remaining-codes count). `beginTotpEnrollment` (creates row, returns currentColor-themed SVG, encrypts secret round-trips, replaces unactivated re-enroll, rejects re-enroll when active). `confirmTotpEnrollment` (activates + mints 10 unique XXXX-XXXX-XXXX codes, rejects bad code, rejects no-enrollment, rejects already-active). `verifyTotpCode` (true / false / inactive). `verifyRecoveryCode` (consumes once, rejects unknown, uppercases input, returns false for non-enrolled). `regenerateRecoveryCodes` (replaces batch + invalidates old, rejects bad TOTP). `getRecoveryCodeStatus` + `acknowledgeRecoveryCodeUse` (use→unack→acknowledged round-trip). `disableTotp` (cascades RecoveryCode rows, rejects bad code). `requiresTotpChallenge` + `markDeviceTotpVerified` (untrusted vs verified device behaviour). `cleanupStaleTotpEnrollments` (drops only unactivated rows older than 24 h ; recent + activated rows survive).
    - [packages/auth/tests/integration/account-lifecycle.test.ts](monark/app/packages/auth/tests/integration/account-lifecycle.test.ts) — 7 cases. Stubs supabase admin's `deleteUser` at the import boundary so we don't hit a real Supabase. `hardDeleteUser` (anonymizes email + clears displayName + nulls avatarUrl, no-op when user missing, anonymizes our row even when supabase fails). `processExpiredDeletions` (only past-grace rows, idempotent on re-run via the `@monark.invalid` filter, supabase-fail still counted as success because the row flipped, returns zero counts on empty table).
  - **`@monark/notifications` integration suite.** [packages/notifications/tests/integration/dispatch.test.ts](monark/app/packages/notifications/tests/integration/dispatch.test.ts) — 14 cases. Forces SMTP to log-only mode (no `SMTP_URL` set) so `sendMail` short-circuits to a structured success ; the unit suite covers nodemailer interaction. `notify` happy path : two Notification rows (EMAIL + IN_APP) for `auth.password-changed`, deliveredAt stamp on EMAIL, body only set on IN_APP. Locale routing : FR user sees an accented FR subject. Dedupe window : same kind+payload within 60 s skipped, distinct payloads not deduped, never deduped across users. Soft-deleted recipient : IN_APP delivers, EMAIL skipped with reason `user-soft-deleted`. Opt-out semantics : non-required-email channel respects the `NotificationPreference` row, but `requiredEmail=true` overrides a disabled SECURITY/EMAIL pref. Unknown recipient returns the empty result without throwing. `notifyMany` aggregates deliveryIds across recipients. Prefs round-trip : `setPreference` upserts, `isChannelEnabled` reads, `resetPreferences` clears, no leak across users.
  - **`@monark/organizations` bootstrap integration.** [packages/organizations/tests/integration/bootstrap.test.ts](monark/app/packages/organizations/tests/integration/bootstrap.test.ts) — 16 cases. Stubs `@monark/feature-flags/server` so the multi-tenant flag is controllable per-test. `getBootstrapStatus` (single-tenant + 0/1/many orgs, multi-tenant always-bootstrapped). `getSingletonOrganization` (null + present). `ensureSingletonOrganizationFromInput` happy path + idempotency (second call returns existing org with `created:false`, original displayName sticks). Guards : multi-tenant short-circuits with `already-multi-tenant`, missing slug or name → `env-not-set`, malformed slug → `invalid-slug` with detail, malformed primaryColor → `invalid-color` with detail, both 3-digit and 6-digit hex accepted. `bootstrapSingletonOrganization` direct : creates + emits, returns existing on a re-call, throws on the >1 org case (operator flipped to single after running multi).
  - **services/api cron-auth refactor + unit test.** Extracted the bearer-token check from [src/server.ts](monark/app/services/api/src/server.ts) into a pure [src/lib/cron-auth.ts](monark/app/services/api/src/lib/cron-auth.ts) helper (`evaluateCronAuth({ authorizationHeader, cronSecret }) → CronAuthResult`). The Express handler now wraps it for the HTTP-status mapping ; behaviour is identical to before. New [tests/cron-auth.test.ts](monark/app/services/api/tests/cron-auth.test.ts) — 6 cases : matching bearer → ok, unset CRON_SECRET → not-configured, mismatched bearer → unauthorized, missing header → unauthorized, bare-secret-without-Bearer-prefix → unauthorized, case-sensitive `Bearer` prefix.
  - **Two final admin e2e specs.**

Per-run unique name so re-runs against a stuck DB don't collide.
    - [tests/e2e/admin-users-invite.spec.ts](monark/app/services/web/tests/e2e/admin-users-invite.spec.ts) — single-tenant mode (org auto-pinned). Sign in as admin → /admin/users → Invite user dialog → fill name + email + first available role → Send → toast → row visible after reload → revoke from the email-encoded aria-label affordance → toast + row gone. Per-run unique email.
    - Both gated by `E2E_FULL_STACK=1` AND require `E2E_ADMIN_EMAIL` + `E2E_ADMIN_PASSWORD`. The seeded admin needs to belong to the singleton org so `adminCreate` doesn't 403.

Six integration suites now cover every package data layer that round-trips through Prisma : rbac (16) + users (17) + organizations data (24) + organizations bootstrap (16) + auth (58) + notifications dispatch (14). Workspace `pnpm typecheck` + `pnpm lint` stay clean. e2e remains gated.

List : excludes anonymized rows ending in `@monark.invalid`, case-insensitive search across email + displayName, cursor pagination + last-page nullCursor, every status filter (active / disabled / pending-deletion), `emailVerified` tri-state, `joinedAfter` filter, ordering by `createdAt desc, id desc`, composing all four filters at once.
    - [packages/organizations/tests/integration/data.test.ts](monark/app/packages/organizations/tests/integration/data.test.ts) — 24 cases. `findById` / `findBySlug` round-trips. `countActiveOrganizations` zero / live-only / multi-row. `findOnlyActiveOrganization` returns null on 0 + null on >1 + the singleton on 1 + ignores soft-deleted. `createOrganizationRow` happy + idempotent on slug + null defaults. `isMember` honors `leftAt`. `findOrgsForUser` joinedAt-ASC ordering, excludes left memberships, excludes soft-deleted orgs. `listOrganizationsForAdmin` excludes soft-deleted + case-insensitive search across displayName + slug + cursor pagination. **`updateOrganizationForAdmin` slug rotation** : updates simple fields without touching the redirect table, rotates slug + records 90-day redirect with the right expiry window, rejects collisions, no-op same-slug doesn't record a redirect, idempotent on multiple rotations through the same old slug, transaction-rolls-back on slug collision (displayName change reverts), throws on missing org id.

New shared [vitest.shared.ts](monark/app/vitest.shared.ts) with v8 coverage provider, lcov + html reporters, and the standard exclude list (re-exports, type-only files, generated code, test fixtures). Every package now has its own `vitest.config.ts` extending the base ; `@vitest/coverage-v8` is wired across the workspace. New `test:coverage` script per package + at the root + as a turbo task ; `pnpm test:coverage` runs the whole monorepo with coverage. Thresholds stay commented-off for the moment — they flip on per package as backlog work closes the gaps. **New test suites** : [`packages/common/tests/{errors,events,result}.test.ts`](monark/app/packages/common/tests/) (28 cases covering every error class, the event-bus single + fan-out + isolation guarantees, and the Result helpers), [`packages/branding/tests/branding.test.ts`](monark/app/packages/branding/tests/) (10 cases : every required field, hex / email / URL validators, template-vars subset purity), and [`packages/notifications/tests/email-shell.test.ts`](monark/app/packages/notifications/tests/email-shell.test.ts) — the regression guard for the 2026-05-05 shell-rendering bug. Iterates every registered `NotificationKind` × `en|fr` (16 combinations) and asserts the rendered HTML / subject / text contain no literal `{{` `}}` substrings, plus that the wordmark + logo URL substitute correctly. Total : 206 tests across the workspace, all green. **CI workflow** : [.github/workflows/ci.yml](monark/app/.github/workflows/ci.yml) split into a fast `verify` job (lint + typecheck + `pnpm test:coverage` + Codecov upload) and a slower `e2e` job that depends on `verify`, boots the Supabase local stack via `supabase start`, runs migrations, installs Chromium + Firefox + WebKit, and runs Playwright. Failure mode uploads the Playwright HTML report as an artefact for 14 days. **Bug found-and-fixed during the rollout** : the existing [`enrich.test.ts`](monark/app/packages/notifications/tests/enrich.test.ts) was asserting against the old `accountLink: ${base}/account` value ; the route-segments work in this same patch had moved it to `/account/danger` (deletion-cancel target) without updating the test. Fixed.

- 2026-05-05: Phase-1 closure pass. Folds in everything that landed since the last CHANGELOG entry without its own line ; phase-2 starts on a clean slate.
  - **Account section moved to real route segments.** `/account?tab=profile|security|notifications|danger` retired in favour of `/account/profile`, `/account/security`, `/account/notifications`, `/account/danger`. New shared layout at [account/layout.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/account/layout.tsx>) hosts the AppBar + sidebar chrome ; per-tab pages export their section content. New client [account-sidebar.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/account/account-sidebar.tsx>) drives off `usePathname()` instead of `?tab=` query state. Deletion-grace lockdown enforced server-side at the layout (allow-list of `/account`, `/account/profile`, `/account/danger` ; everything else redirects to `/account/danger` while in grace). Notifications inbox moved to its own top-level `/inbox` so the path didn't clash with the preferences tab. Every redirect target across the app updated to match : `(authed)/layout.tsx`, `(anon)/signin/actions.ts`, `admin/layout.tsx`, `profile-section.tsx`. Email templates (`auth/totp-{enabled,disabled}.ts`, `auth/new-device.ts`, `account/deletion-scheduled.ts`) + the `accountLink/securityLink/revokeLink` defaults in [enrich.ts](monark/app/packages/notifications/src/server/enrich.ts) now point at the new paths. **i18n** (en + fr) : new `appBar.breadcrumb.{profile, security, danger}`.
  - **Notifications + Apps + UserMenu became right-side drawers.** The bell popover and the apps launcher popover both swapped to `<Sheet>` overlays matching the user-menu pattern. The notifications drawer adds Unread/All tabs, "Mark all as read", per-row dropdown with mark-read/mark-unread/dismiss, and per-kind icons (`KeyRound` / `Laptop` / `ShieldCheck`/`Off`/`Alert` / `AtSign` / `TriangleAlert` / `CheckCircle2`). Pagination via `useInfiniteQuery`. New `notifications.markUnread` tRPC procedure. The standalone `/inbox` page deleted ; the drawer is the canonical surface now. Notification template inapp bodies rewritten to give context instead of repeating the timestamp (the relative-ago in the drawer made the date redundant). User-menu drawer added a banner-backed identity header (user's profile banner / brand-gradient fallback) with the avatar overlap pattern.
  - **Account-action forms moved to modals.** Email change, password change, and the three TOTP flows (enroll / disable / regenerate) now live in `<Dialog>` surfaces instead of inline expansions on the security page. TOTP enroll became a 2-step wizard (scan → verify) with a "Back" return path. The secret in the "Can't scan?" disclosure renders in a copy-button-equipped codeblock matching the recovery-codes view.
  - **Admin RBAC role editor moved to its own pages.** `/admin/rbac/roles/new` (create) and `/admin/rbac/roles/[id]` (edit / delete) replaced the modal editor ; the manager list (`/admin/rbac`) now has a search input, click-the-whole-card-to-navigate rows, and a chevron pointer for visual uniformity with `/admin/users` + `/admin/organizations` (those two also gained chevrons in the same pass). New `rbac.adminGetRole` tRPC procedure for the by-id lookup. The role key is no longer surfaced in either the list or the editor — derived from the name on create. Permission editor : tri-state checkboxes per category, search across keys + descriptions, auto-expand of matching categories while searching.
  - **User-detail roles trimmed.** Assignments render as flex-wrap of removable chips (color-tinted via `Role.color`, X-icon to revoke) with a dashed `+ Add new role` outline chip that opens a small assign dialog ; previously the form lived inline below a separator and pushed the page down on every visit. The sysadmin card on `/admin/rbac` dropped its CLI-instruction footer ; "Since {date}" capitalised properly.
  - **Mobile-aware breadcrumb + non-navigable hrefs.** The AppBar breadcrumb shows only the current segment on `< sm` viewports ; full chain shows on `sm+`. New `ALWAYS_NON_NAVIGABLE_HREFS` (`/admin`) + `SINGLE_TENANT_NON_NAVIGABLE_HREFS` (`/admin/organizations`) sets render those crumbs as muted spans instead of links so the user doesn't waste clicks on pure redirects. New `<RoleNameCrumb>` resolves cuids on `/admin/rbac/roles/[id]` paths.
  - **Email shell rendering fixed.** `renderString(EMAIL_SHELL, …)` was passing a stripped vars object so `{{ appName }}` / `{{ logoUrl }}` / `{{ brandPrimary }}` rendered as literals across every notification email — wordmark, logo, and footer all broken. Fix in [dispatch.ts](monark/app/packages/notifications/src/server/dispatch.ts) spreads the full `vars` into the shell render. The totp-disabled template was reshaped at the same pass to align with the `new-device` footer pattern (muted trailing paragraph at 24px top margin, no border, `#a1a1aa` 13px). Backlog has a snapshot test follow-up.
  - **Dev overlay current-org panel works in single-tenant.** Root fix at the platform layer : `getCurrentOrg` now falls back to the singleton when the session has no `active_organization_id` claim and the deploy is single-tenant. `@monark/organizations` gained `@monark/feature-flags` as a workspace dep. Multi-tenant write path is still missing — flagged in backlog.
  - **Phase-1 cleanup before phase-2.** Dropped four placeholder feature flags (`voting`, `contributions.quantification`, `referral.external-sync`, `onboarding.v2`) ; tests in [packages/feature-flags/tests/](monark/app/packages/feature-flags/tests/) refactored to assert the live flags instead. User-menu drawer cards stripped to neutral "Coming soon" placeholders (no rank labels, no achievement-square grid). Notification preferences UI trimmed to SECURITY + ACCOUNT categories ; ACTIVITY + DIGEST stay in the Prisma `NotificationCategory` enum but the prefs zod schema rejects them so phase-2 can re-enable cleanly. Inbucket SMTP env updated in [services/api/.env](monark/app/services/api/.env) + .env.example to point at port 54325 (the actual listener) instead of 54324 (web UI). **i18n** (en + fr) : dropped `account.notifications.prefs.{categories.{ACTIVITY,DIGEST}, categoryDescriptions.{ACTIVITY,DIGEST}}`, dropped `appBar.userMenu.{achievements, ranks.*}`.

- 2026-05-04: AppBar gains an Apps launcher + a generic auto-derived breadcrumb. New 3x3-grid icon button to the left of the notifications bell opens a [`<AppLauncher>`](monark/app/services/web/src/components/app-launcher.tsx) popover listing every Monark product surface (Core today ; accounting / trading / RWA / etc. layer in via [services/web/src/config/apps.ts](monark/app/services/web/src/config/apps.ts) as those subdomain-hosted apps come online — `external: true` opens in a new tab so the user keeps their session here ; the current app's card carries a "Current" pill and points at "/"). The previous single-segment [`<AppBarTitle>`](monark/app/services/web/src/components/app-bar.tsx) is gone ; the new [`<AppBarBreadcrumb>`](monark/app/services/web/src/components/app-bar-breadcrumb.tsx) walks `usePathname()` segments and renders `{primary nav feature} / {secondary section} / {last subsection}` automatically — e.g. `/admin/organizations/abc` reads "Admin / Organizations / {orgDisplayName}". Deeper paths collapse the middle behind an ellipsis ("Admin / Organizations / … / Danger Zone"). Static segments resolve through the new `appBar.breadcrumb.<segment>` i18n dictionary (kebab-case → Title Case fallback for unmapped segments, no warnings) ; dynamic id segments (anywhere a route lives at `[id]`) dispatch to a per-pattern crumb component that fetches the entity name through tRPC — `<UserNameCrumb>` reads `users.adminGetUser`, `<OrgNameCrumb>` reads `organizations.adminGet`. New `[id]` patterns register by adding one branch in `pickCrumbForSegment` ; everything else flows through `<StaticCrumb>` with no per-page wiring. **i18n** (en + fr) : new `appBar.apps.{triggerAria, aria, title, subtitle, currentBadge, items.core.{name, tagline}}`, new `appBar.breadcrumb.{account, notifications, admin, users, organizations, organization, rbac, setup}` ; dropped `appBar.sections.*` (the `<AppBarTitle>` it fed is gone, the admin-pin label in [primary-nav-menu.tsx](monark/app/services/web/src/components/primary-nav-menu.tsx) now reads from `appBar.breadcrumb.admin`).

- 2026-05-05: RBAC built-ins split into `SYSADMIN` (platform-tier) + `ADMIN` (org-tier). The previous single `ADMIN` role overloaded both scopes ; an assignment's `organizationId` decided which kind of admin you were, which made "two admin permissions" awkward to reason about. The split puts each tier on its own row with locked scope :
  - **`SYSADMIN`** ; platform-only (assignment.organizationId must be null), code-side "all permissions across every org" short-circuit, never UI-assignable. Granted via the new [tools/sysadmin.ts](monark/app/tools/sysadmin.ts) CLI (`grant` / `revoke` / `list` subcommands) or a direct SQL insert. The tRPC `adminAssignRole` and `adminRevokeRole` procedures both refuse the SYSADMIN role with a `FORBIDDEN`, so an org-tier admin can't escalate or de-platform the sysadmin tier through the admin UI. `listRolesForOrg` filters SYSADMIN out, so it never appears in /admin/rbac or in the user-detail role picker.
  - **`ADMIN`** ; org-only (assignment.organizationId must be non-null), same code-side "all permissions in this org" short-circuit. Listed in /admin/rbac and assignable via /admin/users like any other org-tier role. The data layer enforces scope at write time : `assignRole` rejects SYSADMIN with a non-null orgId and ADMIN with a null orgId.
  - Permission resolution short-circuits on either built-in via a shared `BUILTIN_ALL_PERMISSIONS_KEYS` set ; `findActiveAssignments` includes platform-tier SYSADMIN regardless of the requested orgId so a sysadmin passes every org-scoped check, ADMIN inclusion is gated on the assignment's orgId matching the requested one.
  - Schema migration at [packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260505160000_split_sysadmin_admin_roles](monark/app/packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260505160000_split_sysadmin_admin_roles/migration.sql) seeds the new SYSADMIN row and repoints every existing platform-tier (organizationId IS NULL) ADMIN assignment to it. Org-tier ADMIN assignments stay as-is.
  - `validateRoleKey` in the rbac write layer now reserves both `ADMIN` and `SYSADMIN` so operators can't accidentally create a custom role colliding with a code-side guard.

- 2026-05-05: RBAC + admin-users polish round. Six asks landed in one pass : (1) Permission category `members` renamed to `users` for terminology consistency — touches the [permission registry](monark/app/packages/rbac/src/contracts/permissions.ts) + the rbac category-grouping helper + the rbac editor i18n (en + fr). (2) New schema migration [packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260505100000_role_color_invite_displayname](monark/app/packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260505100000_role_color_invite_displayname/migration.sql) adds `Role.color` (optional hex code) and `Invite.displayName` (optional pre-fill for the recipient's name). (3) New shared [`<RoleChip>`](monark/app/services/web/src/components/role-chip.tsx) component renders the role's name with a color-tinted pill + matching dot ; falls back to a neutral muted style when no color is set. Used on /admin/rbac role list and on the user-detail Roles card. The role editor dialog grew a Color input + live swatch preview. (4) Invite dialog grew a "Full name" field that pre-fills the recipient's `displayName` on signup acceptance and addresses them by name in the invite email greeting. The `Invite` row stores it ; `acceptInviteRow` only sets the user's `displayName` when the user hasn't already set their own (preserves customisations on re-invite). (5) `/admin/users/[id]` Roles card now displays the joined organization's display name instead of its raw id (data layer's `findAllActiveAssignments` was extended to include the `organization` row), and the org picker is hidden in single-tenant deploys — the singleton id auto-pins so the form reduces to a single role dropdown. (6) User-list filters refactored : the always-visible filter row collapsed into a single `Filter` icon button beside the search input, opening a `DropdownMenu` with submenus for Role / Status / Joined / Email-verified ; active filters render as compact chips ("Role: Administrator") right after the search bar with an × clear-button each. Powered by the existing shadcn `DropdownMenuSub` + `DropdownMenuRadioItem` ; no new deps. **i18n** (en + fr) : new `admin.rbac.editor.{colorLabel, colorHint}`, new `admin.users.invite.{nameLabel, namePlaceholder, nameHint}`, restructured `admin.users.filters.*` keys (`statusActive` → `status_active` etc. so dynamic `t()` calls land cleanly).

- 2026-05-04: RBAC migrated from a hardcoded `Role` enum to a flexible `Role` + `RolePermission` table-driven model. Operators can now create, edit, and delete custom org-scoped roles via [/admin/rbac](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/rbac/page.tsx>) — name, description, and a permission set picked from a category-grouped matrix. Multi-role-per-user was already supported by `RoleAssignment` ; the UI now surfaces it through the existing /admin/users/[id] role picker (extended to read from the per-org role list). The previous enum's `MONARK_ADMIN` collapsed into the single built-in `ADMIN` row : it lives in the `Role` table with `builtIn: true, organizationId: null`, `key` and `builtIn` are immutable from the API, deletion is hard-blocked, and `hasPermission` short-circuits to `true` for any active ADMIN assignment regardless of `RolePermission` rows so new permissions added to the system later are auto-granted. The `DEVELOPER`, `AMBASSADOR`, `STUDENT` enum values were dropped (they were Monark-specific and the user wants every role beyond `ADMIN` to be operator-defined per org). Schema migration at [packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260504170000_rbac_table_driven_roles](monark/app/packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260504170000_rbac_table_driven_roles/migration.sql) is destructive on populated DBs (drops assignments / invites / flag-overrides for the retired enum values, repoints `MONARK_ADMIN` + `ADMIN` rows at the new ADMIN row) ; user explicitly approved for the starter codebase. Inside the migration, the `Organization → RoleAssignment` `ON DELETE CASCADE` is preserved so the "deleted org / null orgId / accidental platform admin" path still can't happen. The `@monark/rbac` package was rewritten end-to-end : new helpers `hasRoleKey` (replaces enum-based `hasRole`), `requireRoleKey` (replaces `requireRole`), `getAllAssignments` now joins the role row, `findRoleById` / `findBuiltInAdminRole` / `listRolesForOrg` exposed for callers, plus the new write helpers `createRole` / `updateRole` / `deleteRole` (which validate role key shape, reserve the `ADMIN` key, and refuse deletion when assignments exist). New tRPC procedures on the rbac router : `adminListRoles`, `adminCreateRole`, `adminUpdateRole`, `adminDeleteRole`, `adminListPermissions` ; existing `adminAssignRole` / `adminRevokeRole` switched from enum to roleId input. Consumer packages updated to match : `Invite.role` enum → `Invite.roleId` FK ; `FeatureFlagOverride.role` enum → `FeatureFlagOverride.roleId` (still nullable for the global / org / user override variants) ; `FlagScope` carries `roleId` instead of `role` ; `users.adminListUsers` and `organizations.invites.adminListAll` now filter by `roleIds`. Web UI followed : `/admin/users/[id]` Roles card, the user-list role filter, and the invite dialog all read available roles from the live tRPC query against the org's role list (built-in + custom). New events : `rbac.role-created`, `rbac.role-updated`, `rbac.role-deleted`. Permission registry trimmed in [packages/rbac/src/contracts/permissions.ts](monark/app/packages/rbac/src/contracts/permissions.ts) : Monark-specific permissions (`voting:*`, `onboarding:*`, `contributions:*`, `referral:*`) removed ; permissions now carry `description` + `category` so the UI matrix renders without hardcoding. New `rbac:manage-roles` permission added for the new role-CRUD surface. **i18n** (en + fr) : new `admin.rbac.manager.*` and `admin.rbac.editor.*` keys covering the role list, the create/edit dialog, and the four permission categories. **Awaiting** : the operator runs `pnpm --filter @monark/db exec prisma generate` (after stopping any dev server holding the Windows DLL lock) + applies the migration via `prisma migrate dev` on a fresh dev DB.

- 2026-05-04: Tenancy mode + single-tenant boot gate. New feature flag `tenancy.multi-tenant` in [@monark/feature-flags](monark/app/packages/feature-flags/src/contracts/flags.ts) ; defaults OFF, so a fresh deploy is single-tenant unless explicitly flipped. Single-tenant means : exactly one organization is expected, every route gates to a new `/setup` page until that organization exists, and the admin UX collapses around the single-org assumption. New helpers `getBootstrapStatus()` + `bootstrapSingletonOrganization()` + `getSingletonOrganization()` in [@monark/organizations/server](monark/app/packages/organizations/src/server/bootstrap.ts) ; new public tRPC query `organizations.bootstrapStatus` returning `{ mode, bootstrapped, organizationCount }`. New [/setup](monark/app/services/web/src/app/setup/page.tsx) page : status checklist (services / auth / tenancy) that polls every 5s and auto-redirects to home when the system finishes bootstrapping ; "stuck on org" guidance documents the env-var contract verbatim. Bootstrap gate added to both [(anon)/layout.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(anon)/layout.tsx>) and [(authed)/layout.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/layout.tsx>) via shared [isSystemBootstrapped()](monark/app/services/web/src/lib/bootstrap-gate.ts) helper ; runs _before_ the session check so even sign-in / sign-up surfaces redirect to /setup pre-bootstrap. /setup itself lives outside both groups so the redirect doesn't loop. Bootstrap-from-env hook on API service boot ([services/api/src/server.ts](monark/app/services/api/src/server.ts)) reads `INITIAL_ORG_SLUG` / `INITIAL_ORG_NAME` / `INITIAL_ORG_PRIMARY_COLOR` env vars (validated by zod in [lib/env.ts](monark/app/services/api/src/lib/env.ts)) and provisions the singleton organization when missing ; idempotent so a restart on a healthy install is a no-op, attribution lands on `actorId: "system:bootstrap"`. Single-tenant UX : [/admin/organizations](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/organizations/page.tsx>) fast-redirects to the singleton's edit page when mode is single + count is 1 (the list view stays reachable directly via URL for inspection). The "external tool / system" the user mentioned in chat sets the env vars at deploy time ; bridge details land later. **i18n** (en + fr) : new `setup.{eyebrow, title, subtitle, polling, checks.*, stuck.*}`.

- 2026-05-04: Admin invite flow (slice 7 of the admin view ; final slice). Four new admin-gated tRPC procedures on the organizations router under `organizations.invites.*` : `adminList({ organizationId })`, `adminCreate({ organizationId, email, role, appUrl? })`, `adminRevoke({ inviteId })`, and the auto-accept hook `consumePending` (auth-required, no admin gate ; the user is implicitly the target). Plus the underlying primitives [createInvite + acceptInviteByToken + consumePendingInvitesForUser](monark/app/packages/organizations/src/server/invites.ts). New tokens are 32 random bytes hex-encoded ; only the SHA-256 hash is persisted (`tokenHash` per the existing schema), the plaintext is returned exactly once at creation time so the email link can carry it. 14-day expiry. Acceptance is atomic in a single Prisma transaction : marks the row accepted, upserts the OrganizationMembership (handles "left then re-invited" edges), upserts the RoleAssignment using the existing `@@unique([userId, organizationId, role])` compound key. Email is sent through the existing `@monark/notifications/server` `sendMail` primitive with an inline template (no notify-registry registration since invitees aren't users yet — they have no User row to look up locale or prefs against ; defaults to English). `@monark/organizations` now depends on `@monark/branding`, `@monark/notifications`, and `@monark/users`. Auto-accept wired into three flows : the signin server action, the signup server action's auto-signin step, and the `/auth/confirm` route handler ; all three call `organizations.invites.consumePending` after the user lands authenticated, idempotent so retries don't duplicate. UI : [/admin/invites](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/invites/page.tsx>) is a three-card layout — Organization picker, "Send a new invite" form (email + role from ADMIN/DEVELOPER/AMBASSADOR/STUDENT, no MONARK_ADMIN since invites are org-scoped), and the pending list with per-row Revoke. Invite emails point at `/signup?invite=<token>` ; the signup form doesn't yet read the token (auto-accept by email match handles the actual mechanics post-confirmation). **i18n** (en + fr) : `admin.invites.{subtitle, noOrgs, createSuccess, createError, revokeSuccess, revokeError, orgPicker.*, create.*, pending.*}`.

- 2026-05-04: Admin RBAC UI (slice 6 of the admin view). Two new admin-gated tRPC procedures on the rbac router : `rbac.adminAssignRole({ userId, role, organizationId?, reason? })` and `rbac.adminRevokeRole({ assignmentId, reason? })`. Both call into the existing `assignRole` / `revokeRole` server primitives ; the underlying functions already validate the role/orgId pairing (MONARK_ADMIN is platform-only, every other role requires an orgId), so the procedures just gate on rbac and pipe the actor through as `grantedById` / `revokedById` so the audit events carry the right attribution. `@monark/rbac` now has `zod` in its workspace deps. UI : new [admin-roles.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/users/[id]/admin-roles.tsx>) replaces the read-only Roles card on the user detail page ; lists existing assignments with a Revoke button per row, and an "Assign new role" form below that pivots on role selection (MONARK_ADMIN hides the org dropdown and forces `organizationId: null` ; every other role surfaces the org dropdown fed by the same `organizations.adminList` query the orgs page uses, capped at 100 items pre-typeahead). [/admin/rbac](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/rbac/page.tsx>) gets a real (if minimal) page that points operators to the per-user surface ; a global "all assignments" overview is a future enhancement. **i18n** (en + fr) : `admin.users.roles.*` (assignment list + add form + toasts) and `admin.rbac.{subtitle, perUserTitle, perUserSubtitle, perUserCta}`.

- 2026-05-04: Admin organizations CRUD (slice 5 of the admin view). Three new admin-gated tRPC procedures on the organizations router : `organizations.adminList` (cursor-paginated, search by displayName or slug, soft-deleted orgs filtered out), `organizations.adminGet({ id })`, and `organizations.adminUpdate({ id, displayName?, slug?, logoUrl?, primaryColor? })`. Slug rotations record an `OrgSlugRedirect` row with a 90-day expiry inside the same transaction so in-flight links don't 404 ; slug-collision throws `ValidationError` cleanly instead of a Prisma constraint error. Hex-color (`#RGB` / `#RRGGBB`) and slug (`[a-z0-9-]`, 2–60 chars) zod validators added at the procedure boundary. New `organization.updated` event with `changed` array + optional `previousSlug` + actor id ; subscribers can branch on which fields rotated. `@monark/organizations` now has `@monark/rbac` + `zod` in its workspace deps. UI : [/admin/organizations](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/organizations/page.tsx>) renders a debounced search + paginated card list (logo, displayName, slug, primary-color swatch) ; [/admin/organizations/[id]](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/organizations/[id]/page.tsx>) is a single-card edit surface for displayName / slug / primary color / logo upload (onBlur-saved, slug + color get inline regex validation with toast feedback on bad input). Logo uploads route through new admin server action [adminUploadOrgLogoAction](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/organizations/actions.ts>) — same sharp pipeline as user avatars but the file lands at `org-logos/<orgId>/<ts>.webp` in the shared `avatars` Storage bucket. **i18n** (en + fr) : `admin.organizations.{searchLabel, searchPlaceholder, empty, emptySearch, loading, loadMore, detail.*}`.

- 2026-05-04: Admin user management — advanced actions (slice 4 of the admin view). Two new admin-gated tRPC procedures on the notifications router : `notifications.preferences.adminGet({ userId })` and `notifications.preferences.adminSet({ userId, category, channel, enabled })` (plus `adminReset`) ; refactored the existing self-service `get` to share a `resolvePrefsCells(userId)` helper so the matrix shape stays in sync. `@monark/notifications` now has `@monark/rbac` in its workspace deps. New admin server action [adminSendPasswordResetAction](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/users/admin-actions.ts>) triggers Supabase's standard `resetPasswordForEmail` flow against the target's address ; admin never mutates the credential directly, the user always picks their own new password (same email + landing page as the self-service forgot-password flow). The action round-trips through the rbac-gated `users.adminGetUser` first to confirm the caller has admin AND the target exists. Two new cards on [/admin/users/[id]](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/users/[id]/user-detail.tsx>) : [admin-account-actions.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/users/[id]/admin-account-actions.tsx>) (Send password reset button + hint) and [admin-notifications.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/users/[id]/admin-notifications.tsx>) (full toggle matrix mirroring the self-service /account UX). Both auto-disable when the user is in deletion grace. Admin-initiated email change deferred to [docs/todo/backlog.md](monark/app/docs/todo/backlog.md) ; needs design alignment between direct-admin-mutation and a custom pending-email-change-with-user-confirmation flow. Bonus copy fix in the request-deletion confirmation dialog : the description now states explicitly that the user can cancel themselves during the 14-day grace window (closes the "what if they click cancel from their own /account" question). **i18n** (en + fr) : `admin.users.actions.*` (Send password reset card + toasts) ; `admin.users.notifications.{title, subtitle, resetSuccess}` ; expanded `admin.users.dangerZone.requestDialog.description`.

- 2026-05-04: Admin user management — write paths (slice 3 of the admin view). Three new tRPC mutations on the users router : `users.adminUpdateProfile` (admin variant of the self-service profile update ; mirrors the input shape but takes an explicit target `userId`, re-uses the `user.profile-updated` event), `users.adminRequestDeletion` (admin triggers the standard 14-day grace-window flow on a user's behalf, blocks self-targeting since admins delete themselves through `/account`), `users.adminCancelDeletion` (admin cancels a pending grace-window deletion on a user's behalf). Plus one mutation on the auth router : `auth.adminHardDeleteUser` (immediate hard-delete that bypasses the grace window — anonymizes the row + removes the Supabase auth user + emits `user.deleted` in one shot ; lives on auth because Supabase admin teardown is auth's domain ; blocks self-targeting). All four gated by an in-router admin check that calls `adminAssignmentSummary` from `@monark/rbac/server`. UI lives entirely on [/admin/users/[id]](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/users/[id]/user-detail.tsx>) : new [admin-profile-form.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/users/[id]/admin-profile-form.tsx>) (banner, avatar, displayName, bio, locale — onBlur-saved like the self-service section) and [admin-danger-zone.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/users/[id]/admin-danger-zone.tsx>) (request-deletion confirmation dialog, cancel-deletion when in grace, hard-delete with typed-email confirmation matching the self-service dialog UX). Avatar/banner uploads route through new admin server actions [admin-actions.ts](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/users/admin-actions.ts>) — same sharp pipeline as the self-service uploads but the file lands under the _target_ user's storage folder. Profile form auto-disables when the user is in deletion grace (read-only, mirroring the self-service /account lock-down). **i18n** (en + fr) : `admin.users.profileForm.*` + `admin.users.dangerZone.*` (request + cancel + hard-delete copy, error toasts, dialog strings).

- 2026-05-04: Admin user management — read-only foundation (slice 2 of the admin view). Two new admin-gated tRPC procedures on the users router : `users.adminListUsers` (cursor-paginated list with case-insensitive email/displayName search, filters out already-anonymized `@monark.invalid` rows by default) and `users.adminGetUser` (single user + full role-assignment list across every org). Both gated by a new in-router `requireAdmin(ctx.userId)` helper that calls `adminAssignmentSummary` from `@monark/rbac/server` ; `@monark/users` now has `@monark/rbac` in its workspace deps. Rbac side added `getAllAssignments(userId)` + underlying `findAllActiveAssignments` since the existing `getUserRoles` only returns role names without org context, and the admin detail surface needs both. UI : [/admin/users](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/users/page.tsx>) renders a debounced search box + clickable list with avatar, name/email, and "Pending deletion" / "Disabled" badges (tRPC v5 `placeholderData: keepPreviousData` so rows don't blank out mid-typing) ; [/admin/users/[id]](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/users/[id]/page.tsx>) shows identity card, basic fields, and the role assignment list. Read-only for now ; profile edits + admin-initiated deletion ship in slice 3. **i18n** (en + fr) : `admin.users.{title, subtitle, searchLabel, searchPlaceholder, empty, emptySearch, loading, loadMore, badges.*, detail.*}`.

- 2026-05-04: Admin shell scaffolded under `/admin/*` (slice 1 of the admin view ; user CRUD, org CRUD, RBAC, invites still to come). The drawer's admin pin in [primary-nav-menu.tsx](monark/app/services/web/src/components/primary-nav-menu.tsx) is now a filled-orange Button (default variant — `--primary` is the brand orange `#F0870C`, `--primary-foreground` is near-black per [globals.css](monark/app/services/web/src/app/globals.css)) so admin surfaces stand visually apart from regular module entries ; admin is intentionally privileged. Asked-for "no custom CSS" : reuse of the shared shadcn Button (`asChild`) + a Link child. New shared shell at [(authed)/admin/layout.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/layout.tsx>) wraps every `/admin/*` sub-route in AppBar + PageLayout + an AdminSidebar (vertical on `xl+`, horizontal strip on mobile, mirroring the AccountShell pattern). Existing rbac gate (non-admin → `/`) and TOTP-enforcement gate (admin without TOTP → `/account?totpRequired=1`) preserved. Stub pages added at `/admin/{users, organizations, rbac, invites}` ; `/admin` itself redirects to `/admin/users` so admins land somewhere actionable. **i18n** (en + fr) : new `admin.tabs.{nav, users, organizations, rbac, invites}` + section title/placeholder keys for each sub-route.

- 2026-05-04: Account-deletion flow tightened end-to-end. Five gaps closed in one pass : (1) the `/account/delete` page is gone ; the typed-email confirmation now lives in a [`<DeleteAccountDialog>`](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/account/delete-account-dialog.tsx>) launched from the danger tab, which keeps the destructive surface inline with the rest of the danger-zone affordances and removes the only `/account/<sub-route>` that didn't carry tab state. (2) Post-signout the deletion-scheduled banner on `/signin?deletionScheduledAt=…` now formats the date through `useLocale()` + `[locale, "en"]` fallback instead of the runtime default, matching the email-change banner's pattern. (3) Sign-in into a deletion-pending account now lands on `/account?tab=danger` (banner + Cancel button visible immediately) ; [signin/actions.ts](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(anon)/signin/actions.ts>) reads `users.me` after `notifySignedIn` and forwards if `deletedAt` is set. (4) During the 14-day grace window the user is locked into a limited-access shell : the [`(authed)/layout.tsx`](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/layout.tsx>) gate redirects every non-`/account` path to `/account?tab=danger` (reads the resolved pathname via a new `x-pathname` request header set in [middleware.ts](monark/app/services/web/src/middleware.ts), since Next 15 layouts don't expose `pathname` directly) ; [account-shell.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/account/account-shell.tsx>) filters the tab rail to `profile + danger` only ; [profile-section.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/account/profile-section.tsx>) flips every input/button to `disabled` and shows an amber `readOnlyBanner` linking back to the danger tab so the user understands _why_ fields are locked. (5) The 14-day cleanup is now wired : `processExpiredDeletions()` (in `@monark/auth/server`) was already correct but had no caller ; new `POST /cron/process-account-deletions` endpoint on [services/api/src/server.ts](monark/app/services/api/src/server.ts) authenticates via `Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET` and runs the sweep. Deliberately not on the web service since the eslint policy forbids `@monark/*/server` imports there ; any HTTP scheduler (Vercel Cron, GitHub Actions, k8s CronJob, EasyCron) can hit the endpoint. **i18n** (en + fr) : new `account.profile.readOnlyBanner.{title, subtitle, cta}` ; dropped `appBar.sections.deleteAccount`.

- 2026-05-03: Forgot-password flow now requires TOTP for enrolled users — closes the "email compromise == full account takeover" gap. Previously the recovery-link path bypassed TOTP entirely : an attacker who got into the user's inbox (SIM swap → carrier reset → SMS code → email access) could reset the password without ever proving the second factor, undoing the entire purpose of having 2FA on the account. New gate in [`resetPasswordAction`](monark/app/services/web/src/app/auth/reset-password/actions.ts) reads `auth.totp.status` ; non-enrolled users skip the gate, enrolled users either supply a code or get the new `totpRequired` error code so the form can prompt for one. UI side [reset-password-form.tsx](monark/app/services/web/src/app/auth/reset-password/reset-password-form.tsx) routes through the same [`<TotpConfirmDialog>`](monark/app/services/web/src/components/totp-confirm-dialog.tsx) component the in-account password change uses (`scope="passwordChange"`) so the look + retry-on-error UX is identical. Lost-authenticator users still recover via the recovery-code path on `/signin/totp` ; users who lost both go through the out-of-band support path documented in the new [docs/technical-documentation/account-recovery.md](monark/app/docs/technical-documentation/account-recovery.md). **i18n** (en + fr) : new `auth.resetPassword.errors.{totpRequired, invalidTotpCode}`. The dialog itself reuses `account.totp.dialog.passwordChange.*` strings.

- 2026-05-03: Account-recovery strategy documented at [docs/technical-documentation/account-recovery.md](monark/app/docs/technical-documentation/account-recovery.md). Walks through the five-rung ladder (recovery codes → out-of-band support → backup email → trusted-device attestation / passkeys → time-delayed self-service), what's shipped vs deferred, the four cross-cutting design rules every rung respects (TOTP-after-recovery, regen-requires-TOTP, out-of-band as the dead end, notification on every recovery event), and a "what the user sees today" table mapping loss scenarios to recovery paths.

- 2026-05-03: Admin link pinned to the bottom of the primary nav drawer for users with the admin role. Reads `trpc.rbac.isAdmin` (cached `staleTime: Infinity` since admin status doesn't flip mid-session ; defaults to `false` while the query loads so the link doesn't flash for non-admins on first paint). Drawer restructured to `flex-col h-full` so the middle nav region claims `flex-1 overflow-y-auto` (long registries scroll) while the admin pin sits in a `shrink-0` footer with a top border separating it visually. Hidden entirely for non-admins ; the underlying `/admin` route still runs its own server-side rbac gate so this is purely UX. Reuses `appBar.sections.admin` for the label + `lucide-react`'s `ShieldCheck` glyph (matches the totp-banner / admin enforcement chrome).

- 2026-05-03: Primary nav drawer is now driven by a registry instead of a hardcoded list. New [services/web/src/config/primary-nav.ts](monark/app/services/web/src/config/primary-nav.ts) exports a typed `PRIMARY_NAV: PrimaryNavEntry[]` that the application config edits to compose the drawer ; modules opt in by exporting their entry from a `web-nav` (or similar) sub-path and the import lands here. Order in the array IS the order in the drawer. The current starter ships an empty array — the drawer renders "No module registered" instead of the previous Monark-specific placeholder list (Activity / Calendar / Contribution / Projects / Governance / Rewards) which referenced features that don't exist in this codebase. **Each module is responsible for its own secondary nav** (sub-routes inside its own surface) ; the primary drawer is strictly top-level entries.

  Cleanups that came along : [primary-nav-menu.tsx](monark/app/services/web/src/components/primary-nav-menu.tsx) refactored to consume the registry + render the empty state ; [app-bar-title.tsx](monark/app/services/web/src/components/app-bar-title.tsx) stripped of the `/calendar`, `/contribution`, `/projects`, `/governance`, `/rewards` section-key mappings (the sections list now only carries the always-present app pages — account, account-sub-pages, admin) ; the wordmark inside the drawer header reads `{appName}` from i18n instead of the literal "Monark" ; **i18n** (en + fr) dropped `appBar.{sections.{calendar, contribution, projects, governance, rewards}, primaryNav.items.{activity, calendar, …}}` and added `appBar.primaryNav.{brandWordmark, empty}`.

- 2026-05-03: Geolocation is now production-only ; dev mode skips IP + country capture entirely. [`recognizeDeviceAfterAuth`](monark/app/services/web/src/lib/trusted-device-cookie.ts) used to read `x-forwarded-for` + the four edge-geo headers (Vercel / Cloudflare / CloudFront / generic) regardless of environment ; in dev that produced a phone-LAN IP we couldn't translate to anything useful + a null country, so the row stored noise. Now both fields are forced null in dev ; the trusted-device card already gated its "Local development" fallback on `NODE_ENV !== "production" + null IP + null country`, so the visual reads correctly without changes there. UA-CH client hints are still captured in dev (useful for testing the Pixel 7 / Galaxy / iPad model-detection path locally).
- 2026-05-03: TOTP gate on critical operations is now a sequential dialog instead of an inline field. Critical-action forms (change password, change email) had a TOTP `<InputOTP>` shoved into the form alongside the new value + confirmation, which made the form feel like a security checklist and discouraged completion. New shared [`<TotpConfirmDialog>`](monark/app/services/web/src/components/totp-confirm-dialog.tsx) component takes the friction off the main path : non-enrolled users hit Submit and the action runs ; enrolled users hit Submit, the dialog opens, they enter the 6-digit code, the action runs with the code in hand. Server-side rejections (`invalidTotpCode`, `totpRequired`) surface inside the dialog so the user can retry without losing their form input ; non-TOTP errors close the dialog so the user can adjust the form fields the toast pointed them at. Refactored consumers : [password-section.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/account/password-section.tsx>) + [email-change-form.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/account/email-change-form.tsx>). Sign-in TOTP page stays full-page (different context, full-screen layout makes sense there) ; the dialog only replaces the inline-on-form pattern. **i18n** (en + fr) : new `account.totp.dialog.{label, verify, verifying, cancel, default.{title, description}, passwordChange.{title, description}, emailChange.{title, description}}`.

- 2026-05-03: Two security-mail UX gaps closed.

  **Email-change OTP fallback.** Supabase's email-change confirmation puts a 6-digit code in both the old + new address inboxes ; previously there was nowhere to enter it (link was the only path). [email-change-form.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/account/email-change-form.tsx>) now switches to a `<verify>` stage after submit (same pattern as forgot-password) — the form stays mounted with an `<InputOTP>` and prompts for a code from either inbox. New [`verifyEmailChangeOtpAction`](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/account/actions.ts>) server action calls `supabase.auth.verifyOtp({ type: "email_change", token, email })` against the new address first (more common, the user opens the unfamiliar inbox first), falls back to the old address. On rotation completion, mirrors the link path's finalisation : sync the shadow row + `signOut` + redirect to `/signin?emailChanged=1`. When only one side has confirmed, the form stays open with a "first side confirmed, now enter the code from {currentEmail}" prompt. **i18n** (en + fr) : new `account.emailChange.{otpInstructions, otpPendingOther, otpPrompt, otpHalfDone, verify, verifying, errors.{missing, invalidCode, notAuthenticated}}`.

  **Password-changed email copy beefed up.** The "If this wasn't you" path was a single button hand-waving toward `/account` ; now the email surfaces a concrete 3-step incident-response checklist (revoke all sessions → reset password again → enable TOTP if not on yet) before the CTA, so the recipient knows what to actually do when they hit the email. Same wiring as before (`changePasswordAction` / `resetPasswordAction` → `auth.notifyPasswordChanged` → `user.password-changed` event → notifications subscriber → `notify("auth.password-changed", …)`) ; verified intact. **i18n** (en + fr) updated in [packages/notifications/src/templates/auth/password-changed.ts](monark/app/packages/notifications/src/templates/auth/password-changed.ts).

- 2026-05-03: Server-side redirects now build URLs from the request's Host header instead of `request.url`. Symptom : revoking the current trusted device on a phone hitting `http://10.0.0.208:3000` bounced the user through `/auth/sign-out-stale` and ended up at `http://localhost:3000/signin` — a host the phone can't reach. Same class of bug for the TOTP-pending redirect in middleware and the half-dozen redirects out of `/auth/confirm`. Root cause : in several Next.js routing configurations (proxied prod, the `netsh portproxy` we use for LAN-from-phone testing) `request.url` reflects whatever Node sees on its loopback socket, not the public URL the browser typed.

  Fix : new [lib/request-origin.ts](monark/app/services/web/src/lib/request-origin.ts) `getRequestOrigin()` reads `X-Forwarded-Host` + `X-Forwarded-Proto` first (set by reverse proxies that terminate TLS upstream — Vercel, Cloudflare), falls back to plain `Host` + the request protocol, then to `request.nextUrl.origin` if no Host header. Used in [/auth/sign-out-stale](monark/app/services/web/src/app/auth/sign-out-stale/route.ts), every redirect in [/auth/confirm](monark/app/services/web/src/app/auth/confirm/route.ts), and the TOTP-pending redirect in [middleware.ts](monark/app/services/web/src/middleware.ts). Behaviour identical for direct production hits ; the difference only kicks in when a proxy / portproxy is in the loop.

- 2026-05-03: Trusted-device card date format follows the user's app locale + real device model on UA-reduced browsers.

  **Date localisation.** [trusted-device-card.tsx](monark/app/services/web/src/components/trusted-device-card.tsx) `formatRelative` was calling `toLocaleString(undefined, …)` which uses the browser's runtime locale (often the OS default) instead of the user's app preference. Threaded the next-intl `useLocale()` value into the formatter ; now a French-preferences user signing in from an English-OS browser sees French-formatted dates. Falls back to `["<locale>", "en"]` so an unrecognised locale still resolves to something sane.

  **Real device model via User-Agent Client Hints.** Modern Chrome / Edge on Android freeze the User-Agent's device-model field to literally `K` for privacy reasons (UA reduction) ; that's why the card displayed `Device: K` on a Pixel 7. Fix routes through three layers :
  - [middleware.ts](monark/app/services/web/src/middleware.ts) sends `Accept-CH: Sec-CH-UA-Model, Sec-CH-UA-Platform-Version, Sec-CH-UA-Full-Version-List` + `Critical-CH` + `Permissions-Policy: ch-ua-model=(self), …` so participating browsers start sending the real values on subsequent requests.
  - [trusted-device-cookie.ts](monark/app/services/web/src/lib/trusted-device-cookie.ts) reads the hints via `readClientHints()`, strips the wrapping quotes the spec mandates, passes them through the `auth.trustedDevices.recognize` mutation as a typed `clientHints` field.
  - New [`TrustedDevice.clientHints` JSON column](monark/app/packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260503100000_add_trusted_device_client_hints/migration.sql) persists `{ model?, platformVersion?, fullVersionList? }`. `recognizeOrRegister` writes on create + refreshes on every recognized sign-in (treating null as "no fresh signal" so a browser that stops sending hints doesn't wipe the model we already learned). [`toView`](monark/app/packages/auth/src/server/index.ts) now prefers `clientHints.model` over `parsed.device.model` and explicitly treats the literal `"K"` (the UA-reduction sentinel) as missing so we don't accidentally show that placeholder when no hints came through. Falls back to ua-parser-js on browsers that don't implement UA-CH (Firefox, Safari).

  **Apply the migration locally** : `pnpm --filter @monark/db db:migrate:dev --name add_trusted_device_client_hints`. Existing rows have `clientHints = null` and will populate on the user's next sign-in.

- 2026-05-02: All emails now lead with the brand logo + the 6-digit OTP block is centred. Three things landed :

  **Logo at the top.** [EMAIL_SHELL](monark/app/packages/notifications/src/templates/_partials/email-shell.ts) renders a 40×40 `<img src="{{ logoUrl }}">` above the wordmark, where `logoUrl` is composed in [enrichVars](monark/app/packages/notifications/src/server/enrich.ts) as `${appUrl}${BRANDING.logoSrc}` so each request hits a URL the recipient's mail client can actually fetch (works in dev with the Next-served `/monark-logo.svg`, works in prod with the public domain). Wordmark stays directly below the logo so brand identity is still readable when an image-blocking client (Outlook desktop, some Gmail) suppresses the `<img>`. Same logo + wordmark layout in the three Supabase-rendered templates ([confirmation.html](monark/app/supabase/templates/confirmation.html), [recovery.html](monark/app/supabase/templates/recovery.html), [email-change.html](monark/app/supabase/templates/email-change.html)) using `{{ .SiteURL }}/monark-logo.svg` for the same auto-tracking behaviour without needing access to the branding module.

  **6-digit OTP block centred.** Both the prompt label and the codeblock `<td>` get `align="center"` in all three Supabase templates (signup confirm + recovery + email change) ; previously left-aligned which read as visually orphaned next to the centred CTA button above. The mono token + `letter-spacing:0.3em` styling stays.

  **White-label friendliness audit.** Notification templates already use `{{ appName }}` + `{{ brandPrimary }}` for every brand-coloured surface (CTA buttons, header bar, wordmark) ; remaining literal hex codes (`#18181b`, `#3f3f46`, `#a1a1aa`, `#e4e4e7`, `#f4f4f5`) are the standard zinc-neutral ramp every product looks fine in, deliberately left as literals because email clients have spotty CSS-variable support. The three Supabase templates DO still hardcode `Monark` + `#F0870C` in multiple places — Supabase renders templates server-side and can't read `@monark/branding` at render time, so the [white-label retargeting checklist in `packages/branding/README.md`](monark/app/packages/branding/README.md) now explicitly calls these out as "edit by hand when retargeting" alongside the canonical brand-config edit.

- 2026-05-02: Remote dev diagnostics moved into the dev overlay. The standalone `/dev-diagnostics` page is gone ; same probes (web / api / Supabase reachability + auth state + cookies + authed `users.me` round-trip + stale-storage-key self-heal button) now live as a "Remote Dev Diagnostics" section in the dev overlay ([panels/remote-diagnostics.tsx](monark/app/services/web/src/components/dev-overlay/panels/remote-diagnostics.tsx)). Compact text-xs styling matches the rest of the overlay panels. Open with Alt+D or the wrench bottom-right ; the parent `<DevOverlay>` already returns null in production so this never ships to end users either. **i18n** (en + fr) : new `devOverlay.{sections.remoteDiagnostics, remoteDiagnostics.*}` namespace.

- 2026-05-02: Pinned the Supabase auth `storageKey` on every client so the dev-host URL rewrite can't desync sessions. Symptom : LAN-from-phone testing shows `/dev-diagnostics` all-green (web, api, Supabase reachable from phone) but `/account` still loads with empty fields + spinner-locked queries because the browser-side Supabase client can't find the session cookie. Root cause : `supabase-js` defaults `auth.storageKey` to a value derived from the project URL ; the [dev-host-rewrite](monark/app/services/web/src/lib/dev-host-rewrite.ts) changes the URL on the browser (loopback → LAN host) but NOT on the server, so the server writes `sb-127.0.0.1-auth-token` and the browser looks under `sb-10.0.0.208-auth-token`, finds nothing, fires every tRPC call as anonymous, every authed query returns empty.

  Fix : new [lib/supabase/storage-key.ts](monark/app/services/web/src/lib/supabase/storage-key.ts) exporting a single constant (`sb-app-auth-token`, overridable via `NEXT_PUBLIC_AUTH_STORAGE_KEY`) that's now pinned on the [server client](monark/app/services/web/src/lib/supabase/server.ts), the [browser client](monark/app/services/web/src/lib/supabase/browser.ts), AND the [middleware client](monark/app/services/web/src/middleware.ts). All three sides now read + write under the same name regardless of which URL each one is configured with.

  `/dev-diagnostics` extended : new "Auth state on this device" panel shows the cookies the browser can see (names only), whether `getSession()` returned a session, the resolved user id, and the result of an authed `users.me` tRPC call. Surfaces "reachable but no session" vs "session valid but tRPC rejected" vs "everything works but data still empty" — the three different shapes of "data not loading" the previous diagnostic couldn't disambiguate.

  Existing devs : sign out + sign back in once after the upgrade ; old cookies under the URL-derived key won't migrate automatically. Production unaffected (URLs aren't loopback there, no rewrite, key would have been stable anyway).

- 2026-05-02: Dev-LAN diagnostics + one-shot Windows setup script. Two new things to make "phone hits dev PC" work without guesswork :

  **`/dev-diagnostics` page** ([dev-diagnostics/page.tsx](monark/app/services/web/src/app/dev-diagnostics/page.tsx) + [client](monark/app/services/web/src/app/dev-diagnostics/dev-diagnostics-client.tsx)). Returns 404 in production. Open it from the device that's having trouble (typically the phone) at `http://<dev-host>:3000/dev-diagnostics`. Shows the browser's current host, the configured + resolved URL for each service (web / api / Supabase), fetches each one, surfaces the actual error + a hint when something fails ("Connection failed before the server responded — likely Windows Firewall / api not bound to 0.0.0.0 / wrong network", or for Supabase, "containers bind to 127.0.0.1 by default — set up portproxy + firewall rule"). Strictly-dev so it doesn't leak any reachability info in production builds.

  **`tools/dev-lan-setup.ps1`** ([dev-lan-setup.ps1](monark/app/tools/dev-lan-setup.ps1)). Idempotent Windows PowerShell script that auto-detects the dev machine's LAN IP (or accepts `-LanIp 10.0.0.208`), opens Windows Firewall on the **Private** profile only (never public Wi-Fi) for ports 3000 / 4000 / 54321, sets up a `netsh portproxy` from `<LanIp>:54321 → 127.0.0.1:54321` so phones can reach Supabase (which Docker only binds to loopback), and runs `Test-NetConnection` against each port to verify. Tear down with `-Remove`. Replaces the multi-step manual "first netsh, then maybe firewall, then maybe restart Docker" the previous CHANGELOG entry described.

  **`rewriteForCurrentHost` now logs in dev** ([dev-host-rewrite.ts](monark/app/services/web/src/lib/dev-host-rewrite.ts)) — when the URL actually swaps, a `console.info("[dev-host-rewrite] http://localhost:4000 → http://10.0.0.208:4000")` line lands in the browser console so you can verify the swap from your phone (Safari / Chrome both surface this on a remote-debug session).

- 2026-05-02: LAN-from-phone : avatar + banner img URLs auto-rewrite, api binds explicitly to `0.0.0.0`, CORS rejections log clearly, and the scaffolding doc documents the Supabase-binds-to-loopback caveat. Symptom this fixes : after sign-in on phone the user lands on `/account` but every field is empty / spinner-locked because (1) the stored avatar URL is `http://127.0.0.1:54321/storage/...` which the phone can't reach, (2) the api server _might_ not have been bound to all interfaces, (3) every browser-side Supabase / api call falls into a hung connection because the Supabase Docker containers bind to `127.0.0.1` only. Three things landed:

  **Image src rewrite.** [profile-section.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/account/profile-section.tsx>) + [user-menu.tsx](monark/app/services/web/src/components/user-menu.tsx) now run `me.data.avatarUrl` / `bannerUrl` through `rewriteForCurrentHost` before passing to `<img>` / `<AvatarImage>`. Same loopback-swap helper the tRPC + Supabase clients use ; no-op in production where stored URLs already point at the public Supabase host.

  **Explicit `0.0.0.0` bind for the api.** [services/api/src/server.ts](monark/app/services/api/src/server.ts) `app.listen(env.PORT, "0.0.0.0", ...)` — no longer relies on Node's IPv4/IPv6 dual-stack defaulting to "all interfaces", which depending on platform / Node version can silently bind loopback-only.

  **Dev CORS rejection log.** When `NODE_ENV=development`, an origin that isn't in `WEB_ORIGIN` _and_ isn't a private RFC 1918 IP gets a `logger.warn` line on the api side ("CORS rejected non-private origin in dev — add it to WEB_ORIGIN if intentional") so a developer hitting the api from an unexpected origin sees why instantly instead of debugging a generic 500.

  **Supabase containers bind to `127.0.0.1` — manual workaround documented.** This is a Supabase CLI v1 limitation, not something we can fix in app code. [phase-0/project-scaffolding.md](monark/app/docs/features-planning/phase-0/project-scaffolding.md) gains a "Re-binding Supabase to the LAN" subsection with three options : `socat` reverse proxy (simplest), Windows `netsh portproxy`, Docker `-p 0.0.0.0:54321:8000` re-tag, or upgrade Supabase CLI to a version that supports `host = "0.0.0.0"` in `[api]`. Until one of these is applied, browser-side Supabase calls from a LAN device hang regardless of how clean our URL rewrites are.

- 2026-05-02: Password manager autofill (Bitwarden / 1Password / iOS Passwords) detects the auth forms reliably on mobile. Two patterns fixed :

  **1. `autocomplete="username"` instead of `"email"` on sign-in + sign-up** ([signin-form.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(anon)/signin/signin-form.tsx>), [signup-form.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(anon)/signup/signup-form.tsx>)). The HTML spec reserves `username` for the identity field of an authentication form, even when its value is an email address. Mobile password managers key on this exact token to surface saved credentials ; with `email` they often miss the form entirely (the "password field not detected" UX). Pairs with `current-password` on signin / `new-password` on signup.

  **2. Hidden `username` field in password-change + reset-password forms** ([password-section.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/account/password-section.tsx>), [reset-password-form.tsx](monark/app/services/web/src/app/auth/reset-password/reset-password-form.tsx)). Without an associated identity field, password managers either create a brand-new entry with no username or fail to recognise the form as a password change. The hidden input is read from the authed user's email (or the recovery-flow session's email) ; not displayed, not submitted to the server, just there for the manager to associate the new password with the existing entry.

  No changes needed for `auth.totp` codes (`autocomplete="one-time-code"` is correct), recovery codes (free-form text, manager doesn't need to fill them), or the email-change form (`autocomplete="email"` is correct because it's a new email value, not a sign-in identity).

- 2026-05-02: LAN-from-phone testing works without env edits. Five layers had to give for a phone hitting `http://10.0.0.42:3000` to function end-to-end:

  **1. Browser-side URL rewrite.** New [src/lib/dev-host-rewrite.ts](monark/app/services/web/src/lib/dev-host-rewrite.ts) takes any URL whose hostname is loopback (`localhost` / `127.0.0.1` / `::1`) and, when the browser is on a non-loopback host, swaps the hostname to whatever the browser is on while preserving port + protocol. Wired into [trpc-provider.tsx](monark/app/services/web/src/lib/trpc-provider.tsx) and [supabase/browser.ts](monark/app/services/web/src/lib/supabase/browser.ts). Pure no-op in production where the configured URLs aren't loopback ; in dev the phone hits the developer's machine for both api + Supabase instead of its own loopback. SSR / server actions skip the rewrite (they're inside the dev machine, `localhost` resolves correctly).

  **2. Per-request app URL for email links.** New [src/lib/request-app-url.ts](monark/app/services/web/src/lib/request-app-url.ts) reads the request's actual Host header (honouring `x-forwarded-proto` + `x-forwarded-host` for proxied prod) and returns `${proto}://${host}`. Used in [forgot-password/actions.ts](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(anon)/forgot-password/actions.ts>) directly and forwarded through the [signup](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(anon)/signup/actions.ts>) + [resend-confirmation](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(anon)/signup/check-email/actions.ts>) actions to the api. The `auth.signUp` and `auth.requestConfirmationResend` tRPC mutations now accept an optional `appUrl` input that overrides the env-baked default ; falls back to `process.env.APP_URL` when unspecified (cron jobs, scripts).

  **3. CORS for LAN origins.** [services/api/src/server.ts](monark/app/services/api/src/server.ts) gains an `isAllowedOrigin` check : exact `WEB_ORIGIN` matches always pass, AND in `NODE_ENV=development` any RFC 1918 (`10.*`, `192.168.*.*`, `172.16-31.*.*`) or loopback origin is allowed. Production stays strict.

  **4. Supabase redirect allowlist for LAN IPs.** [supabase/config.toml](monark/app/supabase/config.toml) `additional_redirect_urls` now includes wildcards covering every private IPv4 range on port 3000 (`10.*.*.*`, `192.168.*.*`, `172.16.*.*`–`172.31.*.*`) so password-reset / signup-confirm `redirectTo` values minted from a phone's LAN IP are accepted by Supabase Auth.

  **5. Docs + .env notes.** [phase-0/project-scaffolding.md](monark/app/docs/features-planning/phase-0/project-scaffolding.md) + the two `.env.example` files document the LAN testing flow ; no env edits needed, just open `http://<dev-LAN-IP>:3000` from a device on the same Wi-Fi (firewall must allow ports 3000, 4000, 54321).

  Restart `pnpm supabase stop && pnpm supabase start` to pick up the new redirect-URL allowlist (config-file edits aren't hot-reloaded). Restart `pnpm dev` to pick up the api / web changes.

- 2026-05-02: Trusted-device cards show richer device info + suppress loopback noise. `TrustedDeviceView` now ships `deviceVendor`, `deviceModel`, `deviceType` ([packages/auth/src/server/index.ts](monark/app/packages/auth/src/server/index.ts)) parsed once server-side via the existing UAParser call ; mobile UAs typically populate vendor/model (Pixel 7, Samsung A54, iPad), iOS deliberately strips iPhone generation, desktop browsers leave them null. The card ([trusted-device-card.tsx](monark/app/services/web/src/components/trusted-device-card.tsx)) renders a "Device: {vendor} {model}" row when either field is present, picks the glyph from `device.type` (smartphone / tablet / TV / wearable / laptop fallback) instead of the old UA-string sniff, and dedupes vendor-prefix repetition ("Apple iPhone" stays "iPhone").
